# Security review

Status 2026-11-15: first pass done, in two rounds. The attack surface map was split by area and every slice of the
code has been read at least in part by a reviewer; the suspected weaknesses are listed here. Most are as their
reviewer reported them and have not been read a second time; the Status column says which. The twelve High findings
(ten fixes: two pairs are one weakness) are fixed, each with its regression test; its section's **Fix** says how. On
2026-11-16 the Medium findings were fixed in the same way, with follow-ups the same day: all 77 are fixed (SEC-RTE-02
and SEC-OUT-08 finished later the same day), each entry's **Fix** in [Security-Review-Medium](Security-Review-Medium.md)
saying how. The Low findings that were confirmed in code were then fixed in batches the same day, each entry's **Fix**
in [Security-Review-Low](Security-Review-Low.md) saying how, and two batches of the owner's decisions on the ones they
left were carried out after them, and on 2026-11-17 the ones fixed in part were finished: of the 215 Low and Info
findings 159 are fixed and 5 deferred, none refuted, and 51 (12 Low, 39 Info) are as reported. What is left is under
[What is left](#what-is-left).

A third round, 2026-11-17 and 18, read the code the first two did not reach (the "not reached" of the [coverage](#coverage) table and the map files' open questions) in 60 units, and recorded what it found without fixing it: 406 findings with ids from 81 up in each area, 12 High, 89 Medium, 224 Low and 81 Info, none Critical. The lead read the cited lines of every High (8 confirmed, 4 likely) and of 54 of the Medium (23 confirmed, 31 likely); the other 35 Medium, and every Low and Info, are as their reviewer reported them. Round 3's High findings are listed under [the findings that matter most](#round-3); all twelve were fixed on 2026-11-18, each with its regression tests and a reviewer's reading of the fix, and its **Fix** says how; the [counts](#counts) are of all three rounds. [Method](#method) step 7 says how the round was run.

Writing the security proofs ([Proofs-Initial](Proofs-Initial.md), phases P4 and P5, 2026-11-18) found 15 more, each
read in code by a second reader and recorded with the round-3 numbering (SEC-WEB-87 to 93, SEC-RTE-89 and 90,
SEC-MAIL-98, SEC-OUT-92 and 93, SEC-PSF-90, SEC-PFS-112, SEC-PSA-81): one Medium (SEC-WEB-87, second-factor codes sent
at once are not bounded by the lockout), five Low and nine Info. Each entry says "Report: the security proofs". A
sixteenth, a reset password that could become the new password, was fixed the day it was found and is not listed.
Reading BOSS against the standard (phase P7, 2026-11-19) found 11 more (SEC-BAP-136 to 139, SEC-BOS-141 to 146,
SEC-FIL-101): one Medium (SEC-BAP-138, a catalog user's content location read by mr-marble's catalog download), four
Low and six Info; SEC-BOS-146 is the part of SEC-BOS-03 its fix left. The counts include them.

This is the findings register of the security review that the pre-0.1.0 notice in the [README](../README.md) names:
until 0.1.0, findings are recorded openly in this repository. The map of what can be reached is
[Attack-Surface](../docs/security/Attack-Surface.md) and its area files; this document holds what looks wrong with
it. The two secrets plans, [Sealed-Secrets](Sealed-Secrets.md) and [Vault-Instances](Vault-Instances.md), were
reviewed in the same pass and changed in place.

Sections: [method](#method) · [the findings that matter most](#the-findings-that-matter-most) ·
[coverage](#coverage) · [found sound](#found-sound) · [what is left](#what-is-left) · [counts](#counts) ·
[findings by area](#findings-by-area) · [Critical and High findings](#critical-and-high-findings). The Medium
findings in full are in [Security-Review-Medium](Security-Review-Medium.md), and the Low and Info ones in short form
in [Security-Review-Low](Security-Review-Low.md).

## Method

1. Three inventories (framework listeners, routes, outbound clients and child processes; the base and dev components;
   mr-boss and mr-pass) compared what the code has with what the map said.
2. `docs/security/Attack-Surface.md` was split into an index and one file for each area, text unchanged.
3. Reviewers each read one slice of the code and the map text for it, and wrote a report with four lists: what the
   map lacks, what in it is stale, suspected weaknesses, and what was not settled. No reviewer ran anything: every
   item is from reading code, with the lines quoted.
4. For two map files (Web and Data) a second reader checked the report against the code, wrote what held into the map
   and gave each weakness a verdict. The lead read the code for the findings marked "(lead)".
5. In a second round, on a smaller budget, one reader for each remaining slice read the code and wrote what is
   reachable straight into the slice's map file, with the suspected weaknesses in a short report. Those map files and
   findings have had no second reading.
6. The map files whose first-round reports had not been applied (Routes, Admin, Tools, AI, Git, Comms,
   Cluster-Edge, and the PASS frontend and authority) were then written from those reports by one writer each, who trusted an item whose quoted code was
   specific and read the code only where it was not.
7. In a third round (2026-11-17) the code no reviewer had reached was cut into about 60 units, by exposure: what a
   network peer or an anonymous caller reaches in the framework (19 units), what a signed-in user and the clients
   reach (17), PASS (9), and BOSS with the Mantle services behind it (15). One reviewer read each unit and its map
   text, ran nothing, and wrote a report in the form of step 3, with the controls found sound and the map's open
   questions answered from the code. A unit over about 20,000 lines was read from what untrusted input reaches
   inward, and its report says what was read whole, sampled or not reached. The lead read the cited lines of the High
   and most Medium candidates before they were recorded: an entry's **Re-check** says which lines and what was not
   read, and its status says `(lead)`; a Medium with no Re-check, and every Low or Info one, is as its reviewer
   reported it. One writer for each area then wrote the entries and brought the
   area's map file up to date from the reports. Nothing was run in this round; its High findings were fixed after it (2026-11-18). An entry's
   lines, names and settings are those of the commit its unit read: mr-moqui `f6a7de2f9` for units A1 to A19 and B1 to
   B11, before the settings became conf attributes (`plans/archive/Conf-Paths.md` §6 maps each old name to its
   selector); the units after them read the tree of the merge `40c0b234e` or later, and their reports name it. The
   map files use the selectors throughout.

Status values:

- **reported**: as the reviewer wrote it, with the reviewer's own confidence in brackets (confirmed in code, likely,
  to check). Not read a second time. Treat it as a lead to check, not as established.
- **confirmed**, **likely**, **not confirmed (second check)**: a second reader's verdict after trying to refute it.
- **confirmed**, **likely (lead)**: the lead read the lines named in the finding's Re-check.
- **refuted**: read again and found not to hold; listed at the end.

Severity: Critical, an unauthenticated caller gets code execution, another user's session, or bulk data. High, an
anonymous write or read of private data, or a low-privilege user reaching another organization's data or a privileged
action. Medium, needs a signed-in victim's click, an administrator's mistake, or leaks limited data. Low, hardening.
Info, worth writing down. A reviewer's severity is kept unless a second check changed it.

The reports, the briefs the reviewers and writers worked from, the prompts each slice was given, and the script
that builds this document are in `plans/security-review-evidence/`, which is not in the repository's history
(`.gitignore`): a working record on the machine that ran the review.

## The findings that matter most

No finding is rated Critical. These are the ones to look at first; each High one has its section below, each Medium one in
[Security-Review-Medium](Security-Review-Medium.md). Every High one below is fixed (2026-11-15), and every Medium one
(2026-11-16).

- **A disabled account comes back** (SEC-WEB-01, SEC-ADM-01; High; confirmed by the lead). An account disabled for
  good becomes a five-minute lock after four failed sign-in attempts, and a password change writes `disabled=N`. An
  offboarded user who still knows the password signs in again.
- **A link runs script as a Tools administrator** (SEC-TLS-01, and SEC-SCR-01 from the scripts slice; High; confirmed by the lead). `Tools/Data/Export`'s
  `EntityExport` is a `read-only` transition, so it runs on a GET with URL parameters and no session token, and it
  evaluates its `filterMap` parameter as script with the full `ec`, which can start a process on the host.
- **State changes on GET** (SEC-WEB-04, SEC-TLS-04, SEC-RTE-04, SEC-RTE-05, and the same rule behind several others;
  Medium; the rule confirmed by the lead; fixed 2026-11-16: such a GET another site started is refused). The session token is checked on POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE only. A
  transition with actions and the default method runs on a GET with no query string, and a `read-only` transition
  takes URL parameters, on a `SameSite=Lax` cookie. Reported as reachable this way: clearing caches, reloading the
  conf, logout, mailing a rendered report to a given address, the `/resources` file commands, and 57 PASS transitions.
- **Hosted-instance rows start host processes** (SEC-TLS-02; High; reported). System › Instance, which the map did
  not have, runs a stored command as a host process and expands row values as script.
- **Git import reads another repository or a server directory** (SEC-GIT-01; High; reported), and **a pipeline step
  inherits its runner's token** (SEC-GIT-02; High; reported).
- **`GET /email/<id>` marks any message viewed**, anonymous and by a sequenced id (SEC-RTE-01, High, and SEC-MAIL-03;
  reported): a message waiting in the Outbox then leaves the send queries.
- **Any Comms user can host a mail account at any unclaimed address** and receive its mail once inbound SMTP is on
  (SEC-MAIL-01; High; reported).
- **A request's file name is expanded as script when a screen is mailed** (SEC-MAIL-61; High; reported, not run),
  and **mr-marble's party mail screen returns any stored message's body by its id** as inline HTML (SEC-MAIL-63;
  High; reported).
- **Text from records is expanded as script** in more places than the two above (SEC-SCR-05; High; reported): a
  notification's title is expanded, and Mantle builds some titles from record values. `System.getProperty` gave any
  server script every property, the secret ones included (SEC-SCR-03; fixed 2026-11-16: it answers null for a secret).
- **Stored credentials are readable** by an administrator through REST and exports, where the map said write-only
  (SEC-DAT-03, confirmed by the lead; SEC-TLS-05), and a find by request parameters takes conditions on password-hash
  and encrypted fields (SEC-DAT-01). Fixed 2026-11-16: no REST answer or export without `ADMIN_BACKUP_DOWNLOAD` holds
  an encrypted field's value (`<field>IsSet` in its place), and request input takes no condition or order on a password
  field and no order or pattern on an encrypted one.
- **PASS sign-in had no rate limit on the authority** (SEC-PSA-01; Medium; reported; fixed 2026-11-16: 20 sign-ins a
  requester and 10 a user name in ten minutes).
- **`allow-remote` is read by nothing.** The map cited `allow-remote="false"` on about 37 lines as if it kept a
  service unreachable. No Rust code reads the attribute (the service XSD says so). A service is reached from outside
  only through a screen transition, a REST resource, a web script, a token route, the PASS authority's route table or
  an agent tool exposure. The map's Web file says this, and the other files' sentences are corrected or carry a
  note.

### Round 3

All twelve are fixed (2026-11-18), each entry's **Fix** saying how; the Medium, Low and Info ones are not. The two that mattered most shared one cause and gave a signed-in user code execution:

- **A user's own preference is a server write location** (SEC-PFS-82, SEC-BAP-82; High; confirmed by the lead for
  the record files panel, likely for Smithy). Any signed-in user sets any preference at `/apps/setPreference`; 37 Mantle
  and BOSS service files take `mantle.content.root` as the root a file is written under, and others
  `mantle.content.large.root`. The resource layer drops what follows a `#`, so a root naming a server script makes an upload, or
  Smithy's release notes, replace that script. Any staff user reaches it through the record files panel; on PASS any
  account that may store a release does, and it is Critical where a self-registered public account can sign in to
  the server's web.
- **Text from records run as script** (SEC-SCR-81, SEC-BAP-83, SEC-BAP-81; High). A wiki page's editor chooses its
  file type, and a `fancy` page renders with the live context for every viewer; a store manager's promotion
  description, and a queue's acknowledgement text block, are expanded as script. SEC-SCR-05's class again.
- **Data Load and dataset lookups** (SEC-TLS-81, SEC-TLS-82; High; confirmed). Tools > Data > Load's `storeSelected`
  writes any field of any entity a posted list names, restricted ones included (the hosted-instance rows of SEC-TLS-02,
  `UserAccount`'s password fields); a business dataset field's lookup reads any entity and field with authorization
  off, for mr-boss's non-admin data steward.
- **A posted `_isMulti=true` replaces an id a screen pinned** (SEC-RTE-81; High; likely). A transition's
  `service-call` shortcut with the whole context becomes a multi call whose rows take `<name>_0` over the screen's
  value; on mr-marble's My Account a user changes another account's email address. The units of wave D surveyed
  mr-boss and found its eight such transitions unaffected.
- **Accounting and payroll across organizations** (SEC-BOS-81, SEC-BOS-82; High; confirmed). A NACHA return import
  declines or re-authorizes any payment its file names, in any organization; any relationship to an organization
  passes its customer and supplier scope, so AR, AP and treasury users read and change employees' identification
  numbers.
- **A mailed forum post's sender is its From header's display name** (SEC-PFS-81; High; likely), and **a CI runner in
  `--step-user` mode reads a report through a symbolic link as root** and serves it to every reader of the run
  (SEC-GIT-81; High; confirmed).
- Earlier fixes that do not hold on a second path are named in the entries that found them: among them SEC-WEB-07,
  SEC-RTE-07, SEC-ADM-05, SEC-TLS-02, SEC-TLS-11, SEC-MAIL-02, SEC-MAIL-08, SEC-FIL-61, SEC-COM-10, SEC-PSS-28,
  SEC-PSS-62, SEC-BOS-02 and SEC-BAP-08.
- Not weaknesses, but working faults the units met: PASS Login's `startSso` and Settings' `joinSite` are refused as
  unknown form actions; the Shop's card setup and download links end in a framework refusal; `/trk/*` tracking links
  are Not Found on a site host under `framework-routes="refuse"`; and every Marketing text-send test is refused. The
  map files state them as what the code does.

### Round 3

What each area's units read, and what is still not reached (each report's "Read" section has the detail; the map files' open questions hold what is not settled):

- **Web authentication and controls** (units A1, A2): Round 3 read `web/servlet.rs`, `screen/gate.rs`, `screen/frame.rs`, `guard.rs`, `http.rs` and `http/serve.rs`, `admission.rs`, `stream.rs`, `test_peer.rs`, `dav_http.rs`, the recent sign-in services and the SSO and OIDC code (`service/sso.rs`, `service/sso_app.rs`, `moqui-host/src/sso.rs`, `moqui-client/src/sso.rs`, `IdentityNewDevice.js`) whole; still not reached: the guard and HTTP test files, the handlers behind the host routes, `web/reserved.rs`, `screen/static_files.rs`, `ProxyServlet.js` and `AuthFilter.js`, huskarl's JWS verifier and JWKS fetch, `user/factor.rs` and `crate::user::reauthenticate` beyond a grep, and most of `acme/`.
- **Routes, screens and REST** (units A1, A2, A4, B1, B11): Round 3 read the services behind `/rest/s1/moqui` (`moqui.rest.xml`, `service/rest.rs` to its line 830, `user_keys.rs`, and the print, entity sync, system message, mail, user and wiki services the resources reach), `screen/frame.rs`, `web/servlet.rs` and `web/multipart.rs` whole, and the multi mode of a transition's `service-call` shortcut; still not reached: the report engine's bodies, `send#Email`'s template send, `clone#DataDocument`, `ec.search.search` by index, `run_sync`, the system-message queue and consume bodies, `update#Password`, `ProxyServlet.js` and `AuthFilter.js`, `web/reserved.rs`, `screen/static_files.rs`, the six token services, and the application screens that pin an id under a whole-context shortcut.
- **Administration, identity and sign-in** (units B1, B2, B3): Round 3 read whole the configuration, sign-in provider, connection, protocol, readiness and audit services (`Configuration*.js`, `Readiness.js`, `Audit.js`), the Operation, DataTransfer and Backup services with the backup code of `moqui-core`, `moqui-drivers` and `moqui-desktop`, the SSO and app sign-in code (`service/sso.rs`, `sso_app.rs`, `moqui-host/src/sso.rs`) with `IdentityNewDevice.js`, and the `users` resource of `/rest/s1/moqui`; still not reached are `AdminCalls.js`, `Access.js` and `Identity.js` (`heldBeyond`, `clientSummary`), `update#Password`, the Rust services `get#SignInPolicyValues`, `get#ProtocolFacts`, `probe#ProtocolTarget` and `check#Flow`, the ACME, CI, agent and cleanup job service bodies, the mr-mantle and mr-boss transfer targets, connection adapters and owner services, `raw()` in `AdminMount.js`, and huskarl's JWS verifier and JWKS fetch.
- **Tools, System, Monitor and Dashboards** (units B3 to B8, B11): Round 3 read whole the backup services and the embedded dump and restore (`service/backup.rs`, `backup_store.rs`, `moqui-drivers/src/backup.rs`, `moqui-desktop/src/backup.rs`), `moqui-tools` but `capabilities.rs` and `lib.rs` (`sql.rs`, `grid.rs`, `ext.rs`, `schema.rs`, `files.rs`, `etl/`, `jobs.rs`, `activity.rs` and the rest), the component deploy services, the load runner's scripts, targets and service calls, most of the report engine (`lib.rs`, `delivery.rs`, `alert.rs`, `alert_delivery.rs`, `definitions.rs`, `frame.rs`, `git_sync.rs`, `browser.rs`, `incident.rs`, `monitor.rs`, `business.rs`), `moqui-calc` and `moqui-collab`, and the Dashboards screens' transitions; still not reached: the Dashboards template scripts (`tools/template/Report*.js`, `CalcGrid.js`, about 3,900 lines), `CalcGrid.qvue`, `WebrootCalc.js` and the co-editing client, `PrintReport.js` and `WebrootReport.js`, `schedule.rs` in part, `repeat.rs`, `patterns.rs`, `graph.rs` and the regression comparison (sampled), `load_runner/results.rs` and `test_client/http.rs`, `attest::verify` and `write_tree`, the bodies of the ACME, CI and agent job services, the S3 and SFTP clients beyond their constructors, and most System screens.
- **LLM, A2A, MCP and Assist** (units A16 to A19): Round 3 read whole the agent protocols (the MCP server and client, the gateway and its quotas, A2A with push), the run loop and `tools/delegate.rs`, `mcp_client.rs`, `ask.rs`, the read tools, the lazy tools and the canvas validators (`ui/`), `WebrootAssistSandbox.js`, skills, memory, `window.rs`, `assist/read.rs` and `extras.rs`, the screen-session provider, the evals and Dev Assist `hosting`; still not reached: `remote.rs` beyond `link_allowed`, `provider/`, `tools/catalog.rs` (not read again), most of `approval/`, the transition gate and `path_permitted`, `moqui_core::script::standalone`, `check_text`, the terminal Assist pane beyond samples, `Monitor/AgentTraces`, the Skills and Lessons screens, most of the three drawer JavaScript files, and the test files.
- **Git hosting and pipelines** (units A4, A14, A15, B17): Round 3 read the git wire and LFS files whole (`fs.rs`, `fetch_refs.rs`, `mirror.rs`, `fork.rs`, `server/lfs.rs`, `smart_http.rs`, `pack_out.rs`, `upload.rs`, `receive.rs`, `plan.rs`, `path.rs`, `job_fetch.rs`, `review.rs`, `push.rs`), gitoxide's transport, redirect and credential code, the service files `service/hosting.rs`, `rest.rs`, `commit.rs`, `repository.rs`, `review.rs` and `mirror.rs`, the CI services, `ci/file.rs`, `ci/junit.rs`, the runner (`ci/runner/`, `ci_run.rs`), `events.rs` and `file_commit.rs`; still not reached are `reach.rs`, `revwalk.rs`, `db.rs`, `store.rs`, `rules.rs`, `lease.rs`, `ops.rs`, `shared.rs`, the mark and repack bodies of `gc.rs`, `ci/deploy.rs`, `ci/plan.rs`, `ci/registered.rs`, `ci/log.rs`, `ci/token.rs`, `pack_in::read_entries` and `store_entries`, `hosting::write_refs`, the export and import bodies, the SSH host side (`ssh/git.rs`, `ssh/client.rs`) and `moqui_lib::http::RestClient`, and nothing was run.
- **Mail** (units A5, A6): Round 3 read whole the edge mail queue (`smtp_queue.rs`, `edge_mail.rs`), mail DNS, MX delivery, the DMARC and ARC decision (`auth.rs`), `store.rs`, the IMAP and POP sync, IDLE, discovery, the OAuth exchange and the account owner and member services; not reached: `smtp.rs` and `delivery.rs` beyond sampled ranges, `save#Draft` and `send#Draft`, `implementation.rs` as a whole (the SMTP side of an account server by grep only), `report.rs`, how the `imap` crate reads a literal, the `EmailOAuthProvider` seed rows and the scheduler's job lease.
