## Found sound

Checked by a reviewer and reported as holding (none read a second time unless a finding's Re-check says so):

- Static files under the root: no path escapes their directories.
- Entity REST find parameters do not reach SQL unescaped; the page size is 100 by default and at most 1000.
- A bad token on a token route is a 404 and nothing of the screen runs. A screen's own `condition` is not used as
  access control.
- `returnTo` on the sign-in and SSO transitions is same-origin checked and is never taken from a provider's callback.
- Secret and token comparisons in the web sign-in code are constant time or hash lookups, with one exception
  (a single-use code compared in script).
- The SSO account link and group sync keep the bounds the map states (`link_allowed`, `sso_may_give`).
- A browser cannot set a header that a member behind an edge node trusts: the edge node strips and overwrites them.
- Edge pairing codes have 60 bits, a default life of 60 minutes and 10 tries per source in 10 minutes. Only a
  trunk's `authJson` is replicated to an edge node as a credential, and the edge node has no write-back path.
- Chat link previews apply the internal-address guard to the URL, to each redirect and to DNS answers.
- Chat, call and recording services check membership or ownership in the service, attachment downloads included.
  Uploaded HTML and SVG are not served inline. A call guest cannot start recording or captions.
- HTML mail never reaches the application's document: the client cleans it and shows it in a sandboxed frame with no
  script and its own CSP, and remote images are fetched by the browser only. The mailbox services that were read
  check the account in the service. The mail OAuth callback's state is single use, bound to the session and user.
- Smithy and the Forum, in what was read: no read of a private repository or a confidential issue without a view
  check; the opt-out and held-mail tokens are hashed and bound; claiming an imported GitHub identity needs GitHub's own
  endpoints and the numeric id, and moves authorship only.
- The Assist services check the user, `AgentUse` and ownership of every id (the map's open question on this is
  closed). `ConstitutionServices`, granted to every user, checks a sealed permission in each write.
- The PASS operation allow-list: the three copies of the bearer-write list are equal; every routed operation is in a
  schema; a login key reaches only the 18 app-key operations; a delegation is looked up by token digest, client and
  site; a download's location comes from the Mantle service's answer, never from the request.
- The five `llm_*_api_key` properties are inside XML comments in the default conf, and
  `component_deploy_attestation_key` is treated as a secret by its name.
- The audit log holds ciphertext of an encrypted field; cluster cache invalidation carries keys only; the Tools grid
  masks encrypted fields.
- The router and the servlet match use the raw request path with no dot-segment or `//` handling on either side, and a servlet and its filters are chosen from the same string, so no path was found that reaches a host route's handler past its gate or a script servlet without its filter. A rule with `host` never exempts a connection or a ban, a shared ban never bans a trusted proxy, and `/health`, `/metrics`, `X-Forwarded-Proto` and `X-Moqui-Test-Peer` are believed only as the map states (SEC-WEB-11 and SEC-RTE-10 hold). The parent frame (`screen/frame.rs`) and `reauthenticate#User` hold as the map states.
- SSO sign-in: the callback needs the session's pending state and removes it first; `state` is compared in constant time, PKCE S256 is sent unless the provider lists only `plain`, and the ID token's issuer, audience, expiry, `sub` and nonce are required (huskarl 0.11.10; its JWS verifier and JWKS fetch were not read). An account is linked only by a verified email, never a privileged one, and the app sign-in code is single use, hashed and good a minute. The fixes of SEC-ADM-09 and SEC-ADM-11 hold.
- The edge link's acceptance holds as the map states it: a client certificate's signature is verified before its fingerprint is used, an up connection is taken only for an active key, every `Link` call but Probe, Pair and Hello needs an admitted member of the current generation, a member's own key is proved over the TLS exporter value and pinned by a compare and set in one committed transaction, a linked id named with another key is refused, the down connection needs the generation and its 16-byte token compared without an early exit, a front node refuses the edge set's calls, and a forwarded request's host is a strict DNS name with the client's forwarding headers stripped.
- The cluster port serves nothing before the pinned client certificate and keeps the limits the map gives; a delegated call runs on a local node only when its own conf lists it, as `_edge_`, with `auth*`, `_*` and `edgeCaller` dropped from what the edge node sent; and 18 of the 20 delegated call and SIP services have a guard for `_edge_` (the edge node's own instance id, a call whose media node is edge-linked, a fresh digest answer, or the box a call opened by PIN).
- Over SSH, in what was read: the fixes of SEC-FIL-02 (an app password gets the drives over SFTP only) and SEC-FIL-62 (each shell line is audited) hold; "none" is not an offered method; a dialog connection gets one `apps` on a pty and nothing else, and a cluster connection `cluster <topic>` channels only; a key must be the named account's own and active; a disabled, terminated or display account, or one that must change its password, is refused at a key login and at every later command; russh refuses forwarding, agent and unknown channel types; a host program starts from a cleared environment with a close-on-exec pty master; the TUI copies to the clipboard only on a key or mouse action.
- Over DAV and SFTP, in what was read: a path under another user's name is 404 before any handler, and a `.`, `..` or `/` segment is refused in the request path and in a `Destination`; file content is opened only at the location `get#ResourceInfo` answered, and every DAV write goes through a service that checks `canWrite` or `canUpload` itself; names and hrefs are escaped in XML and the HTML listing, and the request XML refuses a DOCTYPE and nesting past 256; a CalDAV PUT matches a UID only inside the calendar named and a client's ATTENDEE, ORGANIZER and VALARM are dropped; a free/busy-only share is no CalDAV collection; SFTP paths cannot leave the root and writes are bounded per user; the SFTP client refuses a changed or mismatched host key; `create#Drive` has no caller that passes a `localPath`.
- Mail delivery and authentication, in what was read: an MX host's internal addresses are dropped before any connection and the session connects to the address that was tested, not to the name again; with an MTA-STS `enforce` policy or `mail_mx_require_tls` the certificate is checked and nothing goes in clear text; the listener's own `Authentication-Results` cannot be extended by what the client wrote, and `rewrite_head` and the delivery's trust test read the authserv-id with the same function; the edge node answers `RCPT TO` from its copies and never calls the local node, and the local node resolves the recipients again from its own accounts and finds its own `mailOut` item by id and direction, never by what the edge node names.
- The mail client's connections and OAuth, in what was read: every IMAP, POP3 and STARTTLS connection goes through `connect_tcp` (one lookup, the checked addresses connected to, never link-local) with the host name checked in TLS, and a plain sign-in is refused without STARTTLS or STLS unless `storeAllowPlain` or loopback; the OAuth sign-in uses PKCE S256, a redirect URI that is never a request's, an ID token whose audience, issuer, expiry and nonce are checked, and logs or answers no token; `transfer#EmailAccountOwner` asks the owner or `EMAIL_ACCOUNT_OWNER_CHANGE`, and `add#EmailAccountUser` level 3 on the account.
- Git LFS: an upload must be the content of its oid, an oid is 64 hex digits and never a path, an object is read only through a `GitLfsObject` row of the request's repository, the `MoquiLfs` token is bound to its repository and operation and authorized again as its user, the upload body is read only after the write check and the size limit, and a job token gets nothing from LFS. On the wire: a want by id must be a commit the readable refs reach and a `have` is acknowledged only for one, a pkt-line and a push's commands are bounded, a gzip body is held to the request limit as inflated, the outbound smart HTTP client follows no redirect and denies internal addresses, and an SSH mirror connects to the checked address with a pinned key.
- Pipelines and git services: a hidden run is read only by the user who triggered it or internally, a cache is never served by the artifact reads, an untrusted run and any merge request's run get no secret, the run check lowers trust and never raises it, a secret's value and an environment's token are never answered, a job token reaches only its own job run, a step's environment holds no runner credential, `ci.xml` and JUnit parsing take no DOCTYPE and at most 256 nested elements, merges read no `.gitattributes` and run no driver or filter command, `merge#MergeRequest` merges the commit it counted approvals for by id, and no route lets a user who is not ADMIN name a git service to call. The fixes of SEC-GIT-10, SEC-GIT-14 and SEC-GIT-16 hold where they were read.
- A2A, MCP and gateway requests, and every agent pool job, run in a context made for the job with no user (`request_pool.rs:476-480`), so no login carries over to the next job on a thread. Every A2A task read, cancel, subscribe, message and push config is by the caller's user id and the `a2a` surface; a push config's wire object carries no token or credentials. An MCP `requestState` is checked by MAC first, in constant time, then for its version, expiry, user, tool and digest.
- In the run loop and the tools that were read: a step logs in as the run's own user, read from the run's row; every READ tool runs under the read fence; an approved write runs once, for the same user, tool call and arguments; a write left running by a stopped node is never run again; a remote's answer, error and tool descriptions reach the model wrapped as untrusted. The fixes of SEC-AI-02, SEC-AI-03, SEC-AI-08 and SEC-AI-12 hold. Every drawer read is keyed by the caller's user id, memory and personal skills are written only by their owner, and a screen session is found and used only by its owner with every write held by the gate.
- SIP, call media, recording and captions (`round3/A9.md`): the fixes of SEC-COM-25 and SEC-COM-28 hold (the nonce MAC covers the source, a foreign nonce is not a failure, the digest `uri` must be the Request-URI, a trunk's address is never counted or blocked). The source of a request is the connection's peer or the Via as rsipstack rewrote it from the datagram's source. Bindings are made only for an authenticated device, within `max_bindings` and the expiry limits. SRTP checks the replay window and the tag before any state change and is never mixed with or downgraded to plain RTP. The in-house G.711 and G.722 decoders index only with masked values. A recording is started only by a host or the link's owner, read only by its owner and the call's hosts, deleted only by its owner; `store#RecordingFile` takes a spool file only under `runtime/tmp/call-recording` after canonicalizing; an end-to-end encrypted call is neither recorded nor captioned; a guest acts only on its own leg.
- Bridges, texts, pushes and XMPP (`round3/A10.md`, `round3/A11.md`): on a full node a webhook for an unknown, adapterless or disabled bridge is 401 before any parse, and the Telnyx (Ed25519), Twilio (HMAC-SHA1 over the configured public URL, never the `Host` header) and Sinch (HMAC-SHA256) signatures are verified before the replay cache and the parse. The media token is an HMAC over bridge, attachment and expiry, and the file must belong to a message delivered to that bridge. Bridge administration checks `ChatBridgeAdmin` in each service body, and `find#Bridges` returns no `configJson`. Sends are checked and counted before the provider, under the day's row lock. No transition of the unit's screens passes the whole context, so a posted `_isMulti` row replaces no pinned id. Every chat push is encrypted to its device, and its recipients come from member rows, never from a caller; a push endpoint must be https and is posted with internal addresses refused and no redirects. In XMPP nothing but STARTTLS is taken on the plain port before TLS, PLAIN is taken only over TLS with an app password scoped to XMPP, SCRAM compares its proof and binding in constant time and refuses a downgrade, a stanza's `from` is overwritten with the session's own JID, a detached session is resumed only by the same user, and rxml refuses DTDs, entity declarations, processing instructions and comments.
- The `moqui` REST root (`round3/B1.md`): below `/moqui` only the last path node needs an `AT_REST_PATH` record, so a signed-in user with no special group reaches only `git` and `hosting`; a path id replaces a request parameter of the same name, and a REST service call is never a multi call. `components/deploy` checks the HMAC before it parses, counts a wrong signature as a failed sign-in and claims each component once, newer only. `keyRevoke` and the other key services touch only the caller's own rows. `get#EntitySyncData` and `put#EntitySyncData` refuse restricted entities (SEC-DAT-08 holds), a stored `filterMap` or `sendUrl` is checked when written, `/rest/sm` compares signatures and URL tokens by `same_text` and spends a replay key once, and `print#ScreenDocument` reads its parameters as JSON only (SEC-SCR-02 holds).
- Request bodies (`round3/A4.md`): a body is bounded before the read and while it arrives, a declared length over the limit is 413 before any read, an upload with no user is held to the body limit outside the open upload paths (a `..` or `%2e` closes one), and a spool file is `create_new` with mode 0600, named by the process and a counter, and removed when the upload is refused or cut short.
- Parsers and spreadsheet output (`round3/A4.md`, `round3/B8.md`): `moqui_lib::node` refuses a DOCTYPE, an `<!ENTITY` and every other declaration, defines no entity, decodes only the five named ones and character references of at most 16 bytes, stops nesting at 256 and interned names at 16,384 a thread, and the DAV reader is that parser. JSON bodies keep serde_json's depth limit of 128 and become decimals with no float. A multipart spool file is `create_new`, mode 0600, never named from the request, and is removed when an upload is refused; a file part's name loses its directories. Images are refused over 30,000 a side in and 16,383 a side or 50,000,000 pixels out, a rich-text image over 8 MiB before its bytes are read. Git archives check names, depth, entries, bytes, disk and concurrency before anything is written, and keep a link only inside. EDI text gives errors, not panics; Code 128 values stay inside the pattern table and barcode SVG text is escaped. The xlsx render mode and the workbook writer write text as a shared string, never a formula; the screen csv mode guards a single widget's value and the headers, the report CSV every text cell, and the BOSS report exports every cell but a plain number in a number column. The IronCalc engine has no file, network, process or service call, bounds evaluation depth, and its xlsx reader expands no XML entity.
- Templates and themes (`round3/B9.md`): the locked sandbox has no `ec`, host object or module loader, only the clock, a discarding `write`, the deadline and three host functions; its global graph and the compiled function are frozen before any template runs, and a caller can only lower its deadline and memory. A theme's limits only tighten the operator's; a package path fits a fixed layout with no `..`, and names are looked up in the package, never as a location; a template's source and every render's output are refused for script, frames, forms, event attributes, `javascript:` and non-image `data:` URLs and unapproved origins; a fragment placeholder needs the page's nonce; rich text goes through `safe_html::clean` and SVG through an allow-list. `runAsSystem` is refused outside a service's own code, `print#ScreenDocument` reads `screenParametersStr` as JSON only (SEC-SCR-02's fix holds), a script's `RegExp` polls the interrupt every 10,000 steps, and a `.fancy` or `.fancy-locked` file under a screen is never sent as a static file.
- Configuration, sign-in providers, connections, readiness and audit (`round3/B2.md`): a read of a connection or a provider returns no secret, and a connection's URL is answered without credentials; the endpoint-change rule clears every stored secret not entered again, and a test sends nothing until it is; a holder of `ADMIN_CONNECTION_MANAGE` alone cannot re-enable a provider it repointed, and enabling one needs `ADMIN_ACCESS_MANAGE`, a passing test of the configuration as it is, the group checks and `HELD_BEYOND`; a mapped or default group is a registered bundle the actor may assign, never privileged or sealed; an existing account is linked by the provider's `email` claim, never a mapped field; no service name comes from a request (an adapter, a message handler, a readiness provider, a refresh or an audit source is taken from its row); a secret setting answers no value and a setting's own `managePermission` is asked; the audit read answers only registered projections and never a value of an encrypted or secret-named field; `call#ChangeSource` and the readiness services are granted without inheritance.
- Operations, transfers and backups (`round3/B3.md`): SEC-ADM-10's fix holds (`call#OwnerService` calls only a service a registration names, its ALLOW is not inheritable, and `is_permitted` takes the artifact's own ALLOW before an inherited one); no recovery kind, action, service or handler name comes from a request; `store#Job` has no parameter for the service, the run-as user, the topic or the dates and writes only registered controls and parameters; a queued call is never listed or read; a message's payload is answered only with the descriptor's `dataPermission` and the owner's word; an uncertain message or mail is never sent again before a person settles it, and sign-in mail is never resent; an import is bounded text and a secret-named column is refused whole; an export file's name and content type come from a fixed table; `BackupServices` take no path, mode or destination, and `run#Backup` is held to one run at a time under a locked ledger row.
- Tools' database and data tools (`round3/B4.md`, `round3/B5.md`): the SQL services ask `SQL_RUNNER_WEB` and all actions on Tools whatever calls them; the confirmation token is random, kept as SHA-256, bound to user, database and text, good ten minutes and spent once; transaction control is refused in every mode, `:name` parameters are bound, one statement runs a call, and a run's connection is closed after it. The grid refuses restricted entities and views over them for read and save, leaves password fields out, masks encrypted ones and takes no condition, filter or sort on a secret field (SEC-TLS-05's fix holds there); export and file locations refuse `..`, `.`, `%` and backslash. The external connection gate (not production, `DATABASE_EXTERNAL`) is on every path and the password is never answered. ETL runs as the user with the Tools frame checked for all actions, an expression needs `GROOVY_SHELL_WEB` at save, check, run and evaluation, and a cron run signs in as the owner only when the owner is a `ServiceJobUser` and takes no location from the job. Deploy from git needs `COMPONENT_DEPLOY`, takes only `https` and `ssh` and passes the URL after `--` with the token in the environment; the attested deploy's gate holds, and SEC-TLS-08's fix holds (the URL is parsed with the `url` crate and the fetch is pinned to the checked address on production). A load run names only a `test/load/*.load.js` of a loaded component and every load service asks `LOAD_RUNNER`.
- Backups, reports, Monitor and CalcGrid (`round3/B3.md`, `round3/B6.md`, `round3/B7.md`, `round3/B8.md`): no `BackupServices` parameter names a path or a destination, the job's dump is `0600` before `pg_dump` writes, the backup directory may not sit inside a cluster's data directory, and the mirror's tar follows no link. A dataset is authorized by the caller's own grant, never an inherited one; its row filter is ANDed in before every request part, context filters take values only from the principal, a drill takes no query, entity or service from the request, and frame transformations and the decimal expressions run no script. A share group is one the saver belongs to or holds the share artifact of; webhook contacts refuse internal addresses and take headers only from named properties; a report file's location is checked against the save roots at create and at each file (SEC-TLS-06's fix holds), and a CSV cell that would read as a formula is prefixed. Ingest keys are 32 random bytes stored as SHA-256 and refused before any read when malformed; replay list and read ask `MONITOR_REPLAY`. CalcGrid checks the profile on each call, reads only an offered entity, leaves encrypted and binary columns out and writes data sheet text as shared strings, and its engine has no file, network or process function; a `collab` join needs the wiki's view right and an update the edit right.
- Dev AIDE, its agent tools, `/devstatic`, the devtools commands and the demo SECA (`round3/B16.md`): file confinement refuses NUL, absolute paths, `..`, a symlink out of the root, `.git`, a first part `target`, the denied runtime names, `.env` copies and other worktrees, and `runtime/conf` below the full tier, on every resolve and for the agent tools' `component://` and absolute names too; a write renames a temporary file over the target, so a dangling symlink at the leaf is replaced, not followed; `propose_edit` asks the extend tier again at accept, binds the approved arguments and refuses a whole-text change over a moved file; branch, tag, stash and remote writes go through `branch_write` at the full tier, and git names refuse a leading `-` and `..` and follow `--`; the group server client sends its token only to the server's own origin, over https or to a loopback IP literal, and refuses a write under a fence; `start#LocalRun` is refused under a fence and from a header login, and the Terminal's host session needs `TERMINAL_HOST`, the server's own origin and, from elsewhere, remote access, https and a recent sign-in; `/devstatic` answers only GET and HEAD, refuses dot names, separators, NUL, `:` and control characters raw and decoded, and serves only a file whose canonical path is under its directory; the LSP has no network listener; `moqui check --agreement` sends the group token only to a configured server the origin remote is on, with no redirect; a sim run hides the after-values of rows the user cannot read.
- The test client and load runs (`round3/B17.md`): a load script run in a server gets only `__moquiTestHost.call`, set before any script line, no browser, the files of its own component (no `..`, no absolute path, no symbolic link followed), and every URL and redirect through `TargetPolicy`, which refuses userinfo, plain http to another machine and anything off the list, with loopback the literal only; `LoadScript::read` takes only a `.load.js` file inside the component; `response.save` and screenshots take a plain file name; `--server` refuses plain http to another machine without `--allow-http`; the offline origin stays under its root; the SSH client needs a pinned host key off loopback; the `X-Moqui-Test-Peer` header is believed only after `test_peer::allow()`, from a loopback peer and client, for an IPv4 loopback value, and is removed before the router; the artifact index refuses a reader with neither `AIDE_READ` or `ADMIN` nor a dev-aide permission on a dev instance, and `apply#XmlOps`, `get#XmlOutline`, `get#SchemaDigest` and `rebuild#ArtifactIndex` need a dev instance; typegen writes fixed file names and the screen lint runs nothing.
- Search (`round3/B10.md`): values in search SQL are bound parameters and a field name reaches SQL only through the store's mapping (an unknown name is `FALSE`); the `tsquery` text is built from alphanumeric words only and `LIKE` patterns escape `\`, `%` and `_`; the trusted filter is ANDed into every statement, the count, the semantic and each hybrid branch and OpenSearch included; a query cannot reach another index, a page ends within 10,000 results, and the query string has no regular expression, fuzziness or slop and nests at most 64 deep; highlight fragments are HTML-escaped around the `<em>` tags; an encrypted or password field is left out of a document's definition and so of the index (SEC-DAT-02 holds), and a `DataDocumentField` expression is written only with `DATABASE_ADMIN`; `search#Universal` shows a type only with view authorization on its primary entity and reads each hit again through the entity facade; the shop search escapes the visitor's text and keeps only published products.
- Caches, the data loader and the database drivers (`round3/B11.md`, `round3/B12.md`): a find the user's filters may change is neither read from nor stored in the shared entity cache, a shared hit is still authorized as a view, a key holds the whole condition, a write evicts conservatively, and a cluster invalidation carries names and keys only; the transaction cache is per thread and frame and a rollback drops its held writes; the loader's XML takes no DOCTYPE and no entity beyond the five and character references and nests at most 256 deep, one streamed element is at most 64 MiB, a zip entry is never written to disk, a restricted load checks every record before any write and refuses every service element, and the loader fetches no URL; the database clients never fall back from TLS to plain text, `verify-full` checks the host name, SCRAM checks the server's signature, MySQL's cleartext plugin is off and a `LOCAL INFILE` request gets an empty file; a server's values decode with bounds checks, request values are always bound, and what the drivers write into SQL is quoted or validated; the fixes of SEC-DAT-06 and SEC-DAT-07 hold; an external connection's stored password is cleared when its host, user or TLS mode changes and is never answered.
- Outbound clients and ACME (`round3/A3.md`): the full guard checks the addresses it connects to (the resolver returns only what `public_addresses` passed, and fails when any one is internal), takes no proxy, and refuses an internal literal before the client is used in `execute`, `open` and `upload` and on each redirect hop; a redirect is followed at most `max` times, to the same host and port unless `any_host`, never from `https` to another scheme; the response limit counts the bytes read, and reqwest is built without gzip or brotli; no `RestClient` sets `danger_accept_invalid_certs`, and a private root is added, not substituted; the row rule denies on production for anything but `Y` (SEC-OUT-02's fix holds), and 6to4, NAT64 and Teredo are judged as SEC-OUT-12's fix says. `setup#AcmeName` takes only a DNS name and an `https://` directory and refuses another tool's directory; the certificate services need an authorization record, granted only through `SYSTEM_APP` to `ADMIN`; an `acme-tls/1` handshake costs at most one read of the rows a second; the account key is derived and refused when unset or `CHANGEME`, and the certificate's key is new for each order. The PASS client checks `grpcs://` certificates and names and bounds its dial, calls and messages; a PASS session handle is an HMAC under a key of its own, good only for its client and site; `probe#ProtocolTarget` connects only to the conf's fixed targets, writes nothing and answers a failure kind.
- Database connections, dialers and processes (`round3/B12.md`, `round3/B17.md`): Postgres and MySQL TLS has no fallback to plain text and `verify-full` checks the host name; an external connection clears its stored password when its host, user or TLS settings change, never answers it, and is changed or tested only by its owner or a `DATABASE_ADMIN` holder; a SIP trunk's TLS is checked for the trunk's own host, never an SRV target, and its credentials answer only its realm; the edge dialer pins the link key on every connection and checks the member list it is given. The PDF browser and the OpenSearch child get a named environment, no `NOTIFY_SOCKET` and a process group of their own; a systemd notify is one datagram of at most 256 bytes; `moq` passes a git URL after `--`, fetches a served `addons.xml` over https, `file` or loopback http only, within 4 MiB and 30 seconds, and checks a Chromium snapshot and a versioned add-on zip against their first download's sum (SEC-OUT-14's fix holds); the compose files publish no OpenSearch port; the desktop app opens externally only http, https, mailto, tel and sip, and keeps its single-instance socket 0600.
- The client crates (`round3/B13.md`): a page's path is always a path on the account's server and the base is fixed per account; a cross-site request, `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-site` included, is refused before the key is added; only fifteen request headers and seven methods pass, never `Cookie`, `Authorization`, `Origin` or `login_key`; the client follows no redirect and keeps no cookie, so the key goes to no other host, and nothing turns TLS verification off; the shell's own paths are its staged files or 404, and the server's documents, frames, scripts, styles and workers are refused on the shell's origin; the outbox takes a chat send only to a comms `/Chat/send` path, at add and again at send, and a mail send only its 22 named fields and the files it names, within count and size limits; store file names are plain, and an account id that could leave the directory is refused; files are written `0600` and renamed and the database is `0600` before SQLite opens it; sign-out and removal revoke the key on the server; SSO uses a 48-byte verifier and a 24-byte state with S256, and the hand-back is taken only by its state; `moqui-embedded` never packs a dot file.
- The phone, the desktop's own files and the shell (`round3/B14.md`, `round3/B15.md`): IPC and the init scripts only in the main frame of the two app origins; room links are an HMAC-SHA256 under a 32-byte key compared in constant time, and call and dial links a nonce taken once within two minutes; a sealed push is opened only with the keys of the account it names, and an unsealed ring is refused for an account with push keys; the chat action receivers are not exported and their intents are immutable but Reply's; no file is backed up or moved to another device; the updater is registered only with a public key and `https://` endpoints, no capability grants its commands, and `install` checks the bytes again; the hidden PDF window has no capability and fails every request its page makes; the service worker keeps only `public` 200 answers of `/libs/` or versioned static files outside the signed-in prefixes; plain chat keeps only `/richTextImage/<id>` images, on the server and in the client. The terminal client cleans every string of a screen model, writes OSC 8 only for an `http`, `https` or `mailto` link of the model, never reads the clipboard, starts no process and writes no file but a download, never over an existing one; with `--server` it refuses plain `http` but to loopback, follows no redirect and keeps its key `0600`.
- The `apps` session's input and output (`round3/B15.md`): an OSC is dropped past 512 bytes and a CSI past 64, and the OSC 11 answer's parse cannot panic; the session loop runs under `catch_unwind` with `panic = "unwind"` in both profiles, so a panic, Revue's own buffer size panic among them, ends one session; the loop refuses downloads; `agent-apps` keeps its size within 40 to 500 by 10 to 500 and takes no size report from its input; OSC 52 carries Base64 only and is written only after a user's action, and the TUI writes no title, DCS, APC or PM.
- The Forum (`round3/C2.md`, `round3/C4.md`, `round3/C5.md`): no Forum operation takes an id of another site or lets a moderator of one category act on another (149 operations, 146 read; `site()`, `category()`, `topic()` and `staff()` check the path's site and that very category, and every other id is found with `p.siteId`); move, merge and split check the destination's moderation again, and `setSanction` refuses oneself and asks a higher rank; staged files are the caller's own of the site, and `getAttachment` serves only a clean file of a post the caller reads; forum mail goes only to watchers, subscribers, the parent's author, mentions and participants, each decided again at send time with the recipient's access, and only to a verified current address; opt-out, invitation and held-mail tokens are 64 random characters kept as a hash and found with the site, and reply tokens are bound to site, party and topic; a reply by mail is posted through `publishPost` as the sender, and spam or an unauthenticated sender is never posted or answered; the Discourse import is an operator's, approved by two people, and its archive expansion keeps regular files only, refuses absolute and parent paths and links, and stops at 4 GiB and a million entries; every Forum page renders through templates that escape, the four `raw()` sites take cleaned or escaped text, and the islands show a post in a sandboxed `m-html-view` with no `v-html`; no mr-pass screen has a `service-call`, so SEC-RTE-81's `_isMulti` does not reach mr-pass; SEC-PFS-02's, SEC-PFS-64's, SEC-PSS-42's and SEC-PSS-43's fixes hold.
- Smithy (`round3/C6.md`): each of the 145 operations of `SmithySchema.js` has a route and runs through `execute` (unknown fields refused, types and lengths bounded, the caller from `ec.user`); `core.repository` and `core.group` refuse another site's row and what the caller may not view, as Not Found, and every other id given with a repository is matched to it; a caller's SSH keys and tokens are its own only; a merge into a protected branch needs a Maintainer and counts the head, method, required contexts, approvals and the agreement, and a force push or a ref delete over git passes every branch rule whatever the role; the agreement gate fails closed, and a signature counts only for the account whose confirmed address authored the line and brought the commit; release assets of artifacts come only from the repository's own trusted runs at the tag's commit; `transferRepository` gives a repository to nobody but the caller or a group it is a Maintainer of; the push gate refuses an unconfirmed or suspended account, an unmounted site and an archived repository.
- The PASS pages other than Smithy (`round3/C1.md`, `round3/C3.md`): `.html.fancy` escapes `& < > " '` and `raw()` is a class no data carries; form widgets escape values and option texts; inline texts write only the request path (still percent-encoded), the session token, mount prefixes (`[a-zA-Z0-9_-]` segments) and one invoice id; every `raw()` site takes text the authority or the frontend cleaned or a builder escaped (Smithy's `h` tag, `Hub.js` `esc`, `WebUtilities.encodeHtml` in `View.js`'s previews); no island uses `v-html`, and island configuration and JSON-LD escape `<`; every `href` from the authority but two is built locally or checked, every redirect goes to `publicPath`, a mount or a checked value, and `returnTo` is kept only as a local path. The shell's CSP has `default-src 'self'` and no `'unsafe-eval'` (SEC-PSF-04's fix holds), and the fixes of SEC-PSF-01 (the local login-key rule) and SEC-PSF-03 (the renamed link parameters) hold. No mr-pass screen has a `service-call`: each transition builds the operation's input from named request values and `View.js` pins `siteId`, so SEC-RTE-81's override has nothing to reach.
- The Shop's pages (`round3/C3.md`): no price, total, tax or discount comes from the client, and the delivery and payment options must be the store's current ones; every write needs the cart's holder and `submitCheckout` refuses any other `via`; a cart capability is never taken from a URL; the checkout relay's amount must equal the frozen amount and its form address is the gateway's constant, every value escaped; `MethodRelay` writes only an Authorize.Net form and `RegistrationRelay` never answers to a return key; orders, addresses, cards and downloads by id are the caller's own or the link's; a page a shared cache may keep carries no session token; a theme sees component placeholders and an allow-listed view, and its assets come only from the active release's pinned digest with a sandboxing CSP. The fixes of SEC-PSS-02 and SEC-PSS-67 hold.
- The PASS authority (`round3/C4.md`): an operation is chosen from the route table and the schemas only (by method and path over REST, by name over gRPC), `run()` refuses one with no route or no schema entry and calls the schema's service, and no two route rows of one method can match one path, so a path names one route and the token-write match and the dispatch name the same operation; the site is the path's or the gRPC message's, and an input `siteId` of another site or a field the schema does not list is 422; a delegation is taken only from a registered client and found by token, client and site together; a registered client calling another site is Not Found (SEC-PSA-05's fix holds); a login key or a header login calls only `APP_KEY_OPERATIONS` with a device key, and no Forum operation is among them; a write needs the session token or one of the listed exceptions over both transports; a download's `location` is taken out of the answer, its type made inert and the sandbox policy added; the SSO front channel refuses a header login and takes `handoffId` alone.
- Who reaches `handle#Call` with `clientVerified` (`round3/C4.md`): the gRPC listener sets it only after `registered(id, secret)`, for a client the transport checked, or for the edge link; the REST servlet never sets it (its call object is fixed fields and the request's values go into `input`); no screen transition, REST resource, PASS route or other servlet names the service, and the one by-name caller found is the `moqui>` command line's `service`, which needs view of the Tools app. SEC-PSA-12 stays as recorded.
- Shop's Mantle services (`round3/C8.md`, and `round3/C3.md` for the checkout's authority side): the store is always the site's Shop mount's; a cart capability reaches only a usable row of the same site, store and currency, each lookup counted first; prices come from `get#StoreProductPrice` at add, change and freeze, and no price, total, tax or discount is read from the client; the freeze re-checks revision, prices, availability, promotion limits, quote, tax and terms under the order's lock, the payment's amount is the order's total, and a placement needs the frozen revision and total; a reference reaches a checkout only with its key's digest, on its site, for a day after completion; the hosted form is refused when the payment's amount is no longer the frozen one; the Authorize.Net webhook's payload status is not applied; the guest order link is issued once, only to the cart's own session; the Authorize.Net profile used for setup and sync is the party's own; a renewal charges only the contract's card, owned by its customer, with recorded consent, which a merge clears, and a cancelled contract is never charged; a store binds and offers only payment gateways enabled for its organization; a stuck placement is resolved only with `COMMERCE_PLACEMENT_RESOLVE` in the store's scope; no Shop page transition is a `service-call` shortcut, so SEC-RTE-81's mechanism has nothing to reach there; the fixes of SEC-PSS-01, -02, -03, -05 and -67 hold.
- The portals, sites and themes (`round3/C9.md`, `round3/C7.md`): the three `TOKEN_WRITES` lists are equal (58 names) and the authority pins `siteId` and refuses fields an operation does not list; every portal finds the organization from the site's mount, and a token (32 to 128 characters, counted before it is read) is compared with it; the self relationship needs the account's own, non-internal party with its current address verified; Sales' and Supplier's links reach only their one issue or order, and the Sales documents' locations are made by the server; Knowledge shows a visitor only a published, unrestricted page of a customer or public source of the organization; the fixes of SEC-PSS-25, -65 and -66 hold; a theme, its drafts, releases, previews and site pages are reached only in the user's stores, publish and restore validate again and bind only the store the site's Shop mount sells, draft and import paths are checked before any write, `ThemeView.contract()` is an allow-list projection and a theme sees components only as placeholders; the scheduled site publication reruns the author's checks as the author; a live redirect is a path of the site; no transition that reaches these services is a whole-context `service-call`, so SEC-RTE-81's mechanism does not reach them.
- The BOSS access model, Admin, Commons, Projects and Comms (`round3/D1.md`, `round3/D2.md`, `round3/D7.md`): no transition of mr-boss can be redirected by SEC-RTE-81's `_isMulti` (Admin has no `service-call` shortcut; Commons, Projects and Comms shortcuts pass an explicit in-map or call a service that checks its own party, and no screen pins an id the service trusts); report datasets are authorized by the user's own records, and `get#OrganizationRowFilter` refuses another `userId`; share links are hashed, expiring and revocable, password tries counted, the grant bound for an hour, `..` and the trash refused; unsubscribe links are hashed and counted, and resubscribe only as the link's undo; invitations keep a hashed 43-character token, refuse privileged and sealed groups at issue and acceptance, re-check the inviter, lock the row and write memberships with authorization on; the intake form counts per project, address and client and refuses HTML, SVG and script files; the clocking kiosk locks a badge after five wrong PINs; Admin's root, `recordOf`, Login as, the bundle drop-downs, `Access.js`'s privileged, delegable and sealed rules, `Lifecycle.js`, `Audit.js`, `Capabilities.js`, `Registry.js` and the Constitution and LogNote screens check their scope; Commons' drives, calendars (shares, publish links, subscriptions), events, Knowledge's access and HTML rules, tasks, contacts' private-contact rule, notifications and requests hold; Projects clients are kept to their client projects and shared items, every client write passes `get#ClientRecordVisibility`, and Comms' attachments, message detail and party searches run under their checks; the fixes of SEC-BOS-08, SEC-ADM-06, SEC-BAP-01 and SEC-GIT-01 hold where these apps reach them.
- People and Accounting (`round3/D3.md`, `round3/D4.md`, `round3/D5.md`, `round3/D6.md`): no People or Accounting transition is a whole-context `service-call` shortcut; a payslip is read only by payroll of its employer or, issued, by its person, and its files by payroll alone; a pay run is read and changed only in the caller's People scope, its approval excludes its preparers and everyone it pays, and a salary change always waits for an approval; compensation and identity document numbers are answered only to payroll or HR of an employer of the person, personal identifications masked and tax types left out; confidential cases are read by their filer and handlers alone; leave, expense, attendance and timesheet decisions refuse the requester and the person; the employee and time imports are bound to the caller's employers; the payroll register and the invoice batch CSV guard formula cells; every Accounting request checks the root's organization, `check#BankAccess` and `check#FinancialAccountAccess` gate every banking read and write, a statement of another account is refused, `reconcile#BankLine` takes only a current candidate, a payment run pays only into the approved account, bank numbers are masked on every Accounting screen and leave whole only in a run's file, holds and bill, budget and run approvals refuse their maker, `allowAccountingSelfApproval` needs `ACCOUNTING_ADMIN` and a reason, `AccountingPolicy` and `InvoiceWorkflowLib` reach every organization only with `ACCOUNTING_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS` (SEC-BOS-02's fix holds there), settlement refuses intercompany parties, journal authoring checks assignments and refuses closing types, period close refuses an ancestor's period, and no service compares or returns a gift card or stored-value code or PIN.
- BOSS Customer Service, Marketing, Warehouse, Manufacturing and Maintenance (`round3/D8.md` to `round3/D11.md`): no transition of these apps is a whole-context `service-call` shortcut SEC-RTE-81 can redirect (the only ones reachable are Commons' four contact transitions under ContactDetail, which pin no id and check their own party, and Notifications' five with explicit in-maps); every Customer Service write checks the case first, `recordOf` refuses a case outside the user's queues and a restricted queue's case to anyone off its team, merge and split stay within one organization and customer, a saved reply is the user's or shared with a group they are in and escapes its variables, and case summaries and intervention reports go only to the customer's own addresses; a Marketing send is read and changed only in its organization, its body is a locked template of declared variables, approval and release exclude its authors, the dispatch rechecks membership, consent, suppression and the frequency cap at each page, each email carries its own unsubscribe token kept as a digest, and tracking links stay on the organization's sites; every Warehouse write calls `check#Record` and Mantle's posting guard (per-user operation keys, records locked in the user's facilities, a move kept in its facility), the Handheld's mounts widen no capability, label requests take only the user's shipments and carrier credentials are never shown; Manufacturing's run guard holds the run, line, operation, stock and facility to the caller's organizations and costs come from standards, WIP and approved time, Maintenance's technicians act only on work assigned to them, and Maintenance's root admits only its own record panel hosts.
- Products, Sales and Purchasing, Insights, the record panels and the integrations (`round3/D12.md` to `round3/D15.md`): the catalog owner scope comes from the user's own memberships and only a system administrator writes a shared product; a price row's access checks the owner, both counterparties, the store and the agreement, an agreement's price is refused to every price editor and import, and the catalog import forces the organization on each row within a 2 MiB bound with a formula-guarded result; `approve#Order` runs its direction, hold and credit checks and every BOSS path to it scopes the order first, no approval is taken by id from the request, refunds go only to the original payments and are capped by what was billed, and Customer Service's and PASS's returns check the order's customer and the shipped quantity; Insights reads its sources through the report engine as the viewer and pins its dashboard's organization, the panels require a file or message to be the record's own and send an active content type as an attachment, `html-view` is framed without script, and the CSV exports guard formula cells; EDI routing comes from the remote's connection, never the payload, an 850 ships only to the partner's own locations, the Shippo token goes only to its API base and its webhooks need a signed remote, SEC-BAP-06's fix holds on the three fetch paths and SEC-BAP-08's on Positive Pay, and Admin's Connections clear a gateway's secrets and disable it until a test passes when its endpoint changes.

## What is left

1. **A second check of the reported findings**, High and Medium first: someone reads the cited lines and looks for
   the guard the reviewer may have missed, then a fix or a refutation. Put off by the owner's decision of 2026-11-15
   until it is needed.
2. **What each slice did not reach.** Round 3 read it (2026-11-17 and 18); its coverage, under [Round 3](#round-3-1) in the coverage section, says for each area what is still not reached, and each map file's open questions what is not settled. Its twelve High findings are fixed (2026-11-18); the Medium ones are the next batch of fixes, and its 35 Medium entries with no Re-check, with every Low and Info one, have had no second reading.
3. **Fixes.** The High findings are fixed (2026-11-15, what they left on 2026-11-16), and the Medium ones (2026-11-16).
   Of the Medium findings none is open, none deferred, none refuted and none fixed only in part. Of the 169 Low and 46
   Info findings, 159 are fixed (152 Low, 7 Info) and 5 Low deferred; none is refuted whole (SEC-GIT-19 and SEC-PSS-28
   are in part); 12 Low and 39 Info are as reported. The first batch of the owner's decisions (2026-11-16) finished 14
   of them: SEC-WEB-05, SEC-WEB-06, SEC-WEB-10, SEC-RTE-17, SEC-ADM-10, SEC-GIT-18, SEC-GIT-19, SEC-COM-20, SEC-DAT-11,
   SEC-PSS-26, SEC-PSS-27, SEC-PSS-28, SEC-PSS-44 and SEC-OUT-13. The second (2026-11-17) finished 7: SEC-MAIL-06,
   SEC-MAIL-07, SEC-CLU-02, SEC-CLU-04, SEC-PSF-04, SEC-PSS-05 and SEC-PSS-23. A third (2026-11-17) finished the five
   fixed in part, SEC-TLS-08, SEC-TLS-17, SEC-COM-21, SEC-DAT-09 and SEC-BAP-06, and the follow-ups of SEC-DAT-11,
   SEC-MAIL-07 and SEC-CLU-02 below. A fourth (2026-11-17), the open ones of most impact: SEC-PSS-66, SEC-SCR-08
   (confirmed: mr-marble's TaskSummary), SEC-ADM-11, SEC-FIL-65 and SEC-TLS-18 (the session id, then the query values).
   A fifth (2026-11-17): SEC-BOS-08, SEC-GIT-13, SEC-FIL-05, SEC-SCR-10, SEC-MAIL-05 and SEC-OUT-14.
   A sixth (2026-11-17): SEC-CLU-06, SEC-PFS-64, SEC-BAP-05, SEC-SCR-09, SEC-RTE-16, SEC-FIL-06 and SEC-PFS-02, and
   four found already fixed by an earlier fix when checked: SEC-DAT-07 (SEC-OUT-06), SEC-RTE-10 (SEC-WEB-11),
   SEC-DEV-03 (SEC-WEB-04) and SEC-MAIL-67 (SEC-MAIL-01). A seventh (2026-11-17): SEC-TLS-14, SEC-TLS-15, SEC-DEV-02,
   SEC-RTE-15, SEC-FIL-63, SEC-PSS-62, SEC-GIT-12, SEC-PSS-45 and SEC-CLI-02.

   Deferred, each for an owner's decision or a design: SEC-COM-19 and SEC-COM-26 (each a design of its own), and
   SEC-CLI-03, SEC-CLI-04 and SEC-CLI-05 (the desktop and Tauri clients, waiting for the desktop build machine; fixed
   2026-11-19, item 9).

   Found while fixing and not fixed: each `RCPT` and delivery sums a mail account's message sizes, with no kept counter
   (SEC-MAIL-07; the owner's call, later work if it is slow).

   Found while fixing and fixed 2026-11-17: on a view with two `Party` members each member is held to the row filter on
   its own (SEC-DAT-11); an edge node answers `452` for an account its local node marked full, the account's users are
   told, and a mail administrator sets `quotaBytes` (SEC-MAIL-07); the content files an edge pairing receives are
   capped (`queue-max-content-files`), and a local node past its own cap tells its administrators (SEC-CLU-02);
   mr-marble's label download streamed a stored URL with no check (SEC-BAP-06); and an import from a caller's http(s)
   source was checked for public addresses and then fetched by a lookup of its own, so a name could change its address
   between them (the Git map, Import and fetch), now fetched under `moqui_git::deny_internal` unless
   `webhook-private-network`.

   Found while fixing and fixed the same day: an account-owned email server written or polled through `PATCH email/servers/{id}`, `/rest/e1` and `servers/{id}/poll` (`update#RelayServer`,
   `sensitive::account_owned_email_server`); IronStone's people search and mention ids outside the chat-target rule
   (`ChatterServices` `search#Users`, `get#UserIdsParties`); an SSH upload-pack's limits held to the whole conversation
   instead of each round; the TURN server's map of pending ALLOCATEs grown by forged requests; and a dependency's TURN
   nonce handling, held at moqui's fork (docs/Build-Test.md, Forks) and tracked privately until disclosed upstream. The other Low and Info findings are
   not fixed. Later work beside them: DANE and TLS-RPT for MX delivery (SEC-MAIL-66 is fixed by `mail_mx_require_tls`
   for the domains it names; the default stays opportunistic).

4. **Round 3's High fixes, 2026-11-18.** Found while fixing them and fixed with them: Data Load's `load`, Browse, Record, the data grid and ETL targets also wrote `UserAccount` password and identity fields (SEC-TLS-81); Customer Service answered a customer's identifications whole (SEC-BOS-82); a NACHA notification of change voided a settled payment (SEC-BOS-81); the forum's mbox import keyed authors by display text (SEC-PFS-81); a wiki screen rendered a request's `pageLocation` and eight mr-marble print screens a request's `templateLocation` as live templates, a space's public URLs were expanded, and comment bodies ran as Vue expressions (SEC-SCR-81); mr-marble's My Account contact info, messages, calendar events, tasks, time entries, request content and expense invoices acted on any record, their ids pinned only in pre-actions or not at all (SEC-RTE-81); personal drives and the wiki blog image were rooted at a user preference (SEC-BAP-82). Changed on purpose: payroll's run approvers and treasury import NACHA returns; payroll bank accounts are kept in People, with a notice to the person and no second confirmation (the owner's decision); an existing account's identity fields change only on the account screens; a report that is a link, and a `#` in any write location or upload name, are refused.

   Found while fixing and not fixed: the framework's `update#UserAccount` asks `check#AccountAction` with `permissionId: 'NONE'`, so a caller that passes another user's `userId` gets no owner check from it; mr-marble's Search expands `DataDocument` titles and link text (administrator records) and its `EditParty`, `EditCustomer` and `EditSupplier` show identifications whole; SEC-BAP-87 (`Knowledge.js` expands `publicPageUrl`) stays open; the CI work directory's predictable scratch names stay with SEC-GIT-98.

5. **Round 3's Medium fixes, 2026-11-18 and 19.** The 54 Medium findings the lead had read were fixed in six batches (four framework, two component), each read by a reviewer and read again after its follow-ups, with SEC-TLS-87, SEC-SCR-84 (already closed by High fixes) and SEC-BAP-87; SEC-CLI-81 is fixed in part. The other 35 Medium findings, with no second reading, are item 12.

   Found while fixing and fixed with them: a shell provider's, or a service's, `disableAuthz()`/`enableAuthz()` could leave the caller's authorization off for the rest of the request (and a failing one, its entity ECA and tarpit switches): `require_authz`, a new `keep_authz` and every service call now hold an `AuthzScope` that puts the caller's state back on any exit (`context/artifact.rs`, `service/call.rs`, `screen/shell.rs`); yrs is patched to decode at most 128 levels of nesting (`patches/yrs`); the CI runner's scratch files are made new and private (SEC-GIT-98); SSH keys, app passwords and device credentials, the SSO flows and SAML, SIP trunks, remote agents and MCP servers no longer keep a secret through a generic write or an endpoint change; Customer Service's customer read masks identifications; the forum's mbox import keys no author by display text; mr-marble's print screens and the Marble and mr-boss search lookups; the share-zip limits are conf settings (`resource-facade/drive-share`); a new `allow-html="app"` cleaner for rich text only signed-in users read.

   Owner's decisions recorded with them: payroll bank accounts are kept in People with a notice and no second confirmation (SEC-BOS-82); a forum escalation needs a second owner (SEC-PFS-83); a different `Sender:` is held only when DMARC does not prove From (SEC-PFS-81); Comms reaches staff by employment, departments of one top-level organization reaching each other (SEC-BOS-98); the every-organization app permissions are sealed, ADMIN inviting into their groups (SEC-BOS-84); `DATABASE_ADMIN` is not counted held with authorization disabled (SEC-SCR-81).

   Found while fixing and not fixed: an endpoint-change rule for `LlmEndpoint` and `EntitySync` (ADMIN only) and for `GitWebhook`, `GitCiEnvironment` and `AgentPushConfig` (their secrets are never sent); a separate origin per account in the desktop and mobile apps (SEC-CLI-81); a search page one short of its size can still answer yes or no for a hidden record (SEC-DAT-82, until the hide rules are in the trusted filter); Marble's record screens and search apply none of `search#Universal`'s per-hit rules; a private forum reply held as spam has no release path; mr-marble's `EditParty`, `EditCustomer` and `EditSupplier` show identifications whole; `store#Membership` has no own-account rule; screen actions and `ec.resource.script()` runs are not scoped like a service call; a proposed screen's actions run with the authorization they ask for (SEC-AI-82); the terminal's input channel is unbounded (SEC-FIL-99); an upload is exempt from the request deadline (SEC-RTE-82); the server runtime never runs its promise job queue (SEC-SCR-83); Mantle's calendar feed import has no size limit of its own (SEC-FIL-82).

6. **The clients' first batch, 2026-11-19.** Of the 25 findings the security proofs' phase P8 recorded (SEC-CLI-101 to
   125, read against OWASP MASVS), fixed: SEC-CLI-105, SEC-CLI-106, SEC-CLI-109, SEC-CLI-111, SEC-CLI-112, SEC-CLI-115,
   SEC-CLI-116, SEC-CLI-121, SEC-CLI-123 and SEC-CLI-124; in part: SEC-CLI-101 (the node in process), SEC-CLI-104 (the
   Gradle wrapper's checksum) and SEC-CLI-120 (a page's `notify`). Found while planning them and fixed with them:
   SEC-CLI-126 (the desktop's node, and a build with an add-on component crate, built crates.io's yrs and stun-proto,
   so SEC-TLS-95's fix was not in the app) and SEC-CLI-127 (the phone's release build kept no R8 rule for two classes
   Rust calls by name). The Kotlin was read and checked by static tests, not compiled or run on a device. Next:
   SEC-CLI-101's sidecar half, SEC-CLI-102, SEC-CLI-107, SEC-CLI-108, SEC-CLI-113, SEC-CLI-117 and SEC-CLI-125; then
   SEC-CLI-103, the rest of SEC-CLI-104, SEC-CLI-110 with SEC-CLI-89, SEC-CLI-114, SEC-CLI-118, SEC-CLI-119, the
   microphone of SEC-CLI-120 and SEC-CLI-122.

7. **The clients' second batch, 2026-11-19.** Fixed: SEC-CLI-101 (its sidecar half: the app makes the setup secret and
   hands it to the sidecar's environment and its window), SEC-CLI-107 (a re-authentication kept on the device key),
   SEC-CLI-108 (an unheard sign-out revoked at a later pass), SEC-CLI-117 (the desktop's screen protection) and
   SEC-CLI-125 (a random UnifiedPush instance); in part: SEC-CLI-102 (on the desktop, whose runtime gives a main frame's
   requests their origin; Android's WebView gives no such sign) and SEC-CLI-113 (the shell's warning of a phone with no
   screen lock; a second Keystore key and an app lock are left). The Kotlin was read, not compiled. Next: SEC-CLI-103,
   the rest of SEC-CLI-104, SEC-CLI-110 with SEC-CLI-89, SEC-CLI-114, SEC-CLI-118, SEC-CLI-119, the microphone of
   SEC-CLI-120, SEC-CLI-122, and SEC-CLI-102 on the phone once a device shows what its WebView gives.

8. **The clients' third batch, 2026-11-19.** Fixed: SEC-CLI-89 and SEC-CLI-110 (camera captures in the app's cache),

9. **The clients' fourth batch, 2026-11-19.** The older client findings and the last parts of fixed ones: fixed

10. **The clients' fifth batch, 2026-11-19.** Fixed: SEC-CLI-07 (the desktop shell's commands carry its load's
   nonce), SEC-CLI-08 (a sign-out may delete a personal account's data), SEC-CLI-90 (the phone's camera, microphone
   and location for its own origins only), SEC-CLI-93 and SEC-CLI-94 (control characters dropped where text becomes a

   (read by the app, to 256 KiB); in part: SEC-CLI-81 (a Chromium profile per account on the desktop; on the phone the

   per user; removing unused ones later, by the owner's decision), the phone's half of SEC-CLI-102 (to check on a

   Left: SEC-CLI-104's Gradle verification, for a machine with the Android toolchain (this one has no SDK platform 37,

   of this batch ran on a device or a built desktop app, and its Kotlin is not compiled.

11. **The clients' sixth batch, 2026-11-19.** SEC-CLI-114 is fixed: the desktop's backups, the nightly one and the
   tray's, are sealed to an age key the app keeps in the OS key store as `pg_dump` writes them (`.dump.age`, no plain
   dump on disk), the tray saves the key to a file, and a restore opens a sealed dump with it. The framework's
   `database-backup@recipient` does the same for a server, and `moqui save-db --recipient` and `load-save --identity`
   take it on the command line. Of the clients' findings, what is open: SEC-CLI-81 on the phone (a webview per
   account), SEC-CLI-92's removal of unused images and SEC-CLI-113's app lock (both designs for later, by decision),
   SEC-CLI-102 on the phone (to check on a device), SEC-CLI-103 (by decision) and SEC-CLI-104's Gradle verification.
   SEC-TLS-97 (the plain dumps' and the backup folder's modes) is left to its own area. Not run on a built desktop
   app: a nightly sealed backup, the tray's key file, a restore from it.

12. **Round 3's last Medium fixes, 2026-11-19.** The 34 Medium findings no one had read a second time, with SEC-WEB-87
   (from the security proofs) and SEC-BAP-138, were fixed in four batches: two framework (SEC-TLS-86, TLS-91, TLS-92,
   TLS-93, TLS-94; SEC-WEB-87, GIT-83, FIL-86, CLI-83, CLI-84 and SEC-BAP-96's framework half) and two component
   (SEC-BOS-86, BOS-87, BOS-88, BOS-89, BOS-90, BOS-92, BOS-93, BOS-94, BOS-96, BOS-97, BOS-101; SEC-BAP-84, BAP-85,
   BAP-86, BAP-88, BAP-89, BAP-90, BAP-91, BAP-92, BAP-93, BAP-94, BAP-95, BAP-96's Mantle half, BAP-138 and
   SEC-PFS-85). Each was checked in code first (none was refuted), read by a reviewer and read again after its
   follow-ups. SEC-BOS-87's first fix broke quick create on about 19 screens, which the registry of record types
   (`RecordLinkType`) put right. Of the Medium findings only SEC-CLI-81 is open, fixed in part.

   Found while fixing and fixed with them: a find's conditions on one field collapsed into the last one, as Java's
   `simpleAndMap` does, so `makeCondition([partyId = me, partyId = X], 'and')` queried only `partyId = X`;
   `find#TimeEntries` gave any party's time for the request's `partyId`, and a search form's input replaced a screen's
   own `econdition` (mr-marble's Notifications and ScheduledFinds, Tools' LLM Inbox listed another user's rows). Every
   condition object, `condition(field, operator, value)`, map entry, search input and `econdition` is now ANDed as
   written; only a second plain `condition(field, value)` replaces (`add_condition`, `condition_field`,
   `crates/moqui-entity/src/entity/find.rs`; merged `4b2e27542`). `ec.user.hasPermission` and `isInGroup` compared a
   grant's and a membership's dates with the clock; they use the effective time, as Java's do, and `EXTERNAL_USER`
   stays on the clock. BAP-86's every-organization reach with no organization active was also in Purchasing, Insights,
   Admin, Warehouse and Manufacturing; each now needs an app permission (`*_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS`, Warehouse's and
   Manufacturing's Mantle's `FACILITY_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS`). A record type with no rule was open to anyone, and a
   component's registration could replace Mantle's rule for one it rules itself.

   Owner's decisions recorded with them: a shipped dataset's seeded fields stay as shipped and a steward only adds
   (SEC-TLS-92); `FACILITY_ALL_ORGANIZATIONS`, like the other apps' every-organization permissions, is ADMIN's alone
   (SEC-BAP-86).

   Settings an operator sets: `mantle@mail-provider-authserv-ids` (the providers whose `Authentication-Results` prove
   a synced mailbox's mail, SEC-BAP-84) and `mantle@forum-inbound-accounts` (the forum's inbound accounts; until set
   the forum reads no route's mail, SEC-PFS-85).

   Filed 2026-11-19 from the notes of items 3 to 5 and 12, each checked in code: SEC-ADM-89 (Marble's Party and
   Customer screens change any account's sign-in fields; `update#UserAccount`'s `NONE`), SEC-DAT-98 (Marble's search
   and record screens skip `search#Universal`'s per-hit rules), SEC-BAP-140 (Marble's identifications shown whole) and
   SEC-RTE-91 (an upload has no total time limit), all Medium; SEC-RTE-92 (a screen action's or script's
   `disableAuthz()` outlives it), SEC-AI-102 (a previewed screen's actions), SEC-ADM-90 (`store#Membership` to oneself),
   SEC-TLS-116 (`LlmEndpoint` and `EntitySync` keep their secret on a new URL) and SEC-BAP-141 (change history beyond
   the fixed field set), all Low. SEC-DAT-82, SEC-FIL-99, SEC-FIL-82, SEC-BAP-93 and SEC-TLS-92 are fixed in part, as
   their own entries say what is left. Checked and not filed: `GitWebhook`, `GitCiEnvironment` and `AgentPushConfig`
   (their secrets are rewritten, audience-bound or never sent), Marble's `DataDocument` titles (only a
   `DATABASE_ADMIN` or a data load writes one since SEC-SCR-05), the promise job queue (SEC-SCR-83; component code
   only), `served` (only rows written before SEC-BAP-138), the CalcGrid minute (a refresh checks scope first) and the
   private forum reply held as spam (a product gap, now given a release path).

   Found while fixing and not fixed: the shipped-quantity ceiling on a return and a check in `settle#Return`
   (SEC-BAP-93); per-viewer field read rules in a record's change history beyond ai-opt-out, encrypted and secret
   fields (SEC-BAP-94); `Content.js` `served` takes any image under a content root, every record's folder included,
   which matters only for rows written before the fix (SEC-BAP-138); and within one CalcGrid minute a member whose
   scope changed still reads rows they could see (SEC-TLS-94). A field of a related entity a dataset steward adds
   widens what the document's other reader groups read from the index (SEC-TLS-92).

| SEC-WEB-03 | `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1303-1310`, `:449-470`, `:1736-1746` | A locked account stays locked while anything keeps trying, its own API key included | Medium | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-04 | `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2667-2672`, `:2070-2098` | Transitions with the default method run their actions on a GET with no token | Medium | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-08 | `Login.xml:116-120` | The first caller creates the administrator | Medium | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-05 | `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:54-61`, `:403-416`, `:514-516` | Several sign-ins keep the session id; the forced password change flow never changes it | Low | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-06 | `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1042-1071`, `:1227-1245` | Logout keeps the session and its attributes; a password change ends nothing | Low | fixed |

| SEC-WEB-09 | `UserServices.xml:404-462` | `reset#Password` rewrites `requirePasswordChange` for any account | Low | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-10 | `runtime/conf/MoquiDevConf.xml:9` | Dev conf: every origin is allowed with credentials | Low | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-11 | `crates/moqui-host/src/http.rs:957-963`, `:1679-1684` | `/health` and `/metrics` trust a loopback TCP peer | Low | fixed |
| SEC-WEB-12 | `crates/moqui-core/src/user/totp.rs:89-106` | Second factor details: a TOTP code works more than once; code sends are not limited in the login flow | Low | fixed |

## Critical and High findings

Each as its reviewer wrote it. "Not confirmed" is what the reviewer did not trace; "Re-check" is the second reading, where there was one.

#### SEC-WEB-01. An account disabled for good comes back by itself: four failed logins and a wait, or a password change

High; confirmed (lead); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-15: a failed login never gives an account disabled for good a lock time (`write_failed_increment`, `increment#UserAccountFailedLogins`), and a password change ends only a tarpit lock (`update_password_fields`, `update#PasswordInternal`, whose `disabled` has no default now). Tests: `user/login_tests.rs`, `framework/test/ClosedAccounts.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs:1303-1310`, `:1511-1520`, `:1736-1746`; `runtime/components/base/framework/service/org/moqui/impl/UserServices.xml:150-164`, `:166-283`, `:290`, `:382`; `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot/Login.xml:105-114`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, `POST /Login/login` (or any request with Basic credentials or the account's login key) four times, then a sign-in five minutes later with the account's own password or key; or anonymous `POST /Login/changePassword` or `/ChangePassword/changePassword` with the account's `username`, `oldPassword` and a new password.
- **What happens:** `disable#UserAccount` leaves `disabled = Y` and `disabledDateTime` null, which the login gate, the per-request session refresh and the map (old line 51) treat as closed for good. Route 1: each attempt on the disabled account is counted; the fourth sets `disabledDateTime = now`, which turns the permanent state into the five-minute lock, and after the window `still_disabled` is false, so the right password or key signs in and `finish_login` writes `disabled = N`. Route 2: `update#Password` never reads `disabled`, and `update#PasswordInternal` writes `disabled` from its parameter, whose default is `N`; `Login/changePassword` then signs the user in. `reset#Password` does not read `disabled` either, so the reset password works as `oldPassword` for route 2.
- **Evidence:** `login.rs:1305` `if still_disabled(account, now) { ... increment_failed(facade, &user_id)?;`; `login.rs:1742` `let when = if disable { now_value() } else { row_value(row, "disabledDateTime") };`; `login.rs:1515-1519` `let Some(disabled_at) = field_millis(account, "disabledDateTime") else { return true; }; ... disabled_at.saturating_add(window_ms) > now`; `login.rs:1389-1392` `if failed_or_disabled(account) { ... fields.push(("disabled", Value::string("N")));`
- **Evidence:** `UserServices.xml:290` `<parameter name="disabled" default-value="N"/>`, `:382` `<field-map field-name="disabled" from="disabled"/>`, `:278-279` `update#PasswordInternal ... in-map="{userId: userId, newPassword: newPassword, newPasswordVerify: newPasswordVerify}"`; `login.rs:1258` `("disabled", Value::string("N")),` in `update_password_fields`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. Whether an application service that closes an account also sets `terminateDate` (which neither route undoes) was not traced; `revoke#AccountAccess` "leaves the account enabled" by the map. `Security.eecas.xml` has no rule on `UserAccount`.
- **Suggested fix:** Never write `disabledDateTime` on an account that is disabled with none (in `write_failed_increment` and `increment#UserAccountFailedLogins`), or keep the administrative disable in its own field. Make `update#Password` and `reset#Password` refuse a disabled or terminated account, and stop `update#PasswordInternal` and `update_password_fields` from writing `disabled` unless the caller asked.
- **Re-check:** Read `gate_account`, `still_disabled` and the failed-login update in `crates/moqui-core/src/user/login.rs`: an account with `disabled=Y` and no `disabledDateTime` gets `disabledDateTime` set once the count passes `max-failures`, and `still_disabled` then lets it through after `disable-minutes`. Read `update#PasswordInternal` in `UserServices.xml`: `disabled` defaults to `N` and is written. looked for a guard on the permanent state in `gate_account`, `increment_failed`, `update#Password`, `update#PasswordInternal`, `reset#Password` and `internal_login`; none. `terminateDate` is the one state neither route undoes (`terminated` in `gate_account`). `reset#Password` does not read `disabled` either, so a mailed reset password works as `oldPassword` for route 2. Not read: which application services set `terminateDate` when they close an account.

#### SEC-RTE-01. `GET /email/{id}` lets anyone move mail to Viewed

High; reported (likely); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-15: the tracking image's path is the message's view key, `{id}.{tag}`, the tag an HMAC of the id under a key derived from `session_cookie_key` (`web::email_view_key`, `emailViewKey` in the mail render context, mr-marble's `EmailLayout.html.fancy`); a bare id or a wrong tag changes nothing, and only an `ES_SENT` message becomes `ES_VIEWED`. Test: `web/tests.rs` `view_email_message_sends_the_png_and_marks_a_sent_message_by_its_key`.
- **Where:** `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1306` (`mark_email_message_viewed`); `runtime/components/base/webroot/screen/webroot.xml:277`
- **Reached by:** anonymous, `GET /email/<emailMessageId>` (or `<id>.png`), one request per id.
- **What happens:** The id is the only check, and `emailMessageId` is a plain `type="id"` key (a sequenced id). Any `EmailMessage` not already `ES_VIEWED` is updated to `ES_VIEWED` with `receivedDate` set to now, with authorization disabled. The entity-auto status check allows it from `ES_READY` (the Outbox), `ES_SENT` and `ES_RECEIVED`, so a caller who walks the id range takes queued mail out of `ES_READY` and rewrites the status and received date of received mail.
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-core/src/web/mod.rs:1322` `.eq("emailMessageId", Value::string(&id)).disable_authz().one()?;` and `:1334` `("statusId".to_string(), Value::string("ES_VIEWED")), ("receivedDate".to_string(), crate::user::now_effective_value()),` and `:1341` `disable_authz: true,`
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/BasicEntities.xml:670` `<moqui.basic.StatusFlowTransition statusFlowId="Default" statusId="ES_READY" toStatusId="ES_VIEWED" transitionName="View"/>` (and `:673` from `ES_SENT`, `:675` from `ES_RECEIVED`)
- **Evidence:** `runtime/components/base/framework/entity/BasicEntities.xml:538` `<field name="emailMessageId" type="id" is-pk="true"/>`
- **Not confirmed:** that ids are in fact sequential in every create path; whether the mail client still sends a message that left `ES_READY` this way (the send code in `crates/moqui-mail` was not traced); whether a rate limit of web protection covers `/email`.
- **Suggested fix:** Put a keyed MAC of the id in the pixel URL and refuse without it, and apply the change only from `ES_SENT` without touching `receivedDate` of other statuses.

#### SEC-ADM-01. A disabled account re-enables itself with a password change

High; confirmed (lead); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-15: `update#Password` refuses an account disabled for good or past `terminateDate` unless the caller has `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `reset#Password` makes no reset password for one, and the password path writes `disabled` only to end a tarpit lock. Tests: `framework/test/ClosedAccounts.spec.js`, `user/login_tests.rs`.
- **Where:** `UserServices.xml:166-282` (`update#Password`), `:284-385` (`update#PasswordInternal`)
- **Reached by:** anonymous, `POST /ChangePassword/changePassword` or `/Login/changePassword` with the account's `username`, its current (or unexpired reset) password and a new password; a second factor code too when the account needs one.
- **What happens:** `update#Password` never reads `disabled` or `terminateDate`. It calls `update#PasswordInternal` with three parameters, whose `disabled` parameter defaults to `N` and is written to the account. An account disabled for good (`disable#UserAccount`: `disabled` Y, no `disabledDateTime`, the state the map says ends sessions and refuses logins) becomes enabled, and `/Login/changePassword` then signs it in. `reset#Password` does not read `disabled` either, so the reset mail is sent for a disabled account and its reset password works as the old one. `terminateDate` still refuses the login.
- **Evidence:** `UserServices.xml:278-279` `<service-call name="org.moqui.impl.UserServices.update#PasswordInternal" out-map="context" in-map="{userId: userId, newPassword: newPassword, newPasswordVerify: newPasswordVerify}"/>`; `:290` `<parameter name="disabled" default-value="N"/>`; `:382` `<field-map field-name="disabled" from="disabled"/>`; `:396-402` `disable#UserAccount` writes `{userId: userId, disabled: 'Y', disabledDateTime: null}`; `base/webroot/screen/webroot/Login.xml:110-111` `<if condition="updateSuccessful"><set field="loginSuccess" from="ec.user.internalLoginUser(username)"/></if>`
- **Not confirmed:** not run. Whether Mantle's offboarding sets `terminateDate` as well as `disabled` was not read (it would still stop the login).
- **Suggested fix:** In `update#Password` refuse an account that is disabled for good or past `terminateDate` unless the caller has `ADMIN_PASSWORD`; do not pass or default `disabled` from the password path (write it only in `create#UserAccount`).
- **Re-check:** Same reading as SEC-WEB-01 (`UserServices.xml` `update#PasswordInternal`: `disabled` defaults to `N` and is written).

#### SEC-TLS-01. Export's `filterMap` runs a caller's script, by GET, with no script permission

High; confirmed (lead); fixed.

- **Fix:** Fixed 2026-11-15: `filterMap` is parsed with `JSON.parse`, never evaluated, and `EntityExport`, `exportDocuments` and `AutoEditMaster` `export` are no longer `read-only`, so they take a POST with the session token and refuse URL parameters. Tests: `tools/test/EntityExport.spec.js`, `framework/test/client/SecurityScreens.spec.js`.
- **Where:** `runtime/components/base/tools/screen/Tools/Data/Export.xml:18` and `:54-61`; `crates/moqui-core/src/script/oxide/resource.rs:246-272`
- **Reached by:** a user with update on the Tools app (`ADMIN` by seed, whether or not in `ADMIN_ADV`) who requests, or is sent to, `GET /apps/tools/Data/Export/EntityExport?entityNames=<any entity>&filterMap=<script>`. A link on another site is enough: the request is a top-level GET, the cookie is `SameSite=Lax`, and no token is asked.
- **What happens:** `filterMap` is passed to `ec.resource.expression`, which compiles and runs it in the running engine with the transition's own `ec`. The script can call `ec.artifactExecution.disableAuthz()` and then any service or entity operation, and `ec.resource.runTokenCommand('<program> <args>')`, which starts a process on the server. The sealed `GROOVY_SHELL_WEB` permission, which ETL asks for an expression and which no seed group holds, is not asked here. By the guide's click rule this is Medium; it is rated High because it is also a step for an `ADMIN` outside `ADMIN_ADV` from the Tools grant to script and process execution on the host.
- **Evidence:** `Export.xml:18` `<transition name="EntityExport" begin-transaction="false" read-only="true" authz-action="update">`; `:56` `const filter = ec.resource.expression('(' + filterText + ')', null)`
- **Evidence:** `oxide/resource.rs:247-252` `fn expression(...) { let expression = text_arg(scope, 0)?; ... with_additional(scope, true, |scope| evaluate(scope, &expression))`; `:270-272` `run_here(scope, &source, hash, super::compiled::FILE_NAME, Some(expression))` ("compiled through the runtime's cache and executed while the engine is lent, so its names are the caller's", `:68-69`)
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-core/src/screen/render.rs:2076` `if transition.read_only || !runs_actions(transition) { return None; }`; `:2668-2673` GET is not token-checked
- **Evidence:** `crates/moqui-core/src/script/host/tools.rs:721`, `:2615` (`runTokenCommand` → `Command::new(program).args(words)`); `crates/moqui-tools/src/etl/access.rs:167` `has_permission("GROOVY_SHELL_WEB")` (the check ETL makes and Export does not)
